- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are William, leader of The Netherlands (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Netherlands
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Sea Beggar, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Unit: Goedendag, Replacing Pikeman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Doelen, Replacing Constabulary (Requires technology: Banking)
        - Unique Improvement: Polder (Can only be built on Marsh tiles or on featureless Lake/Coast tiles adjacent to at least 3 land tiles. Polders built on water can be traversed by land units without embarkation, as if they are land tiles.); Requires technology: Guilds
        - Civilization Ability: +4 Gold for each unique Luxury Resource you import or export from/to other Civilizations and City-States, scaling with Era. Can import duplicate Luxuries from Civilizations which count toward Monopolies. +1 Gold and Culture from Luxury Resources.
      - Leader: William
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      
      ## Policies
      - Entrepreneurship (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from Workshops.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from every Mine, Quarry, and Lumber Mill.
        - * +25% Yields when you expend Great Merchants or Great Engineers for their Instant Yield abilities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Mercantilism (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Science and Culture from Markets, Caravansaries, Customs Houses, Banks, and Stock Exchanges.
        - * Cities earn +10 Science when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 6
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 9
        - WarBias: 8
        - HostileBias: 10
        - WarmongerHate: 10
        - NeutralBias: 5
        - FriendlyBias: 8
        - GuardedBias: 10
        - AfraidBias: 4
        - DiplomaticBalance: 10
        - Friendliness: 10
        - WorkWithWillingness: 10
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 10
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 5
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 3
        - MinorCivWarBias: 2
        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 2
        - Meanness: 8
        - Neediness: 2
        - Chattiness: 8
        - DeceptiveBias: 7
        - Rationale: India's surprise naval declaration of war and successful destruction of our Dreadnought and Paratrooper require an even more aggressive defensive posture. Increase WarBias to 8 (from 7) to prioritize offensive planning against Indian naval units near our coast. Increase Boldness to 9 (from 8) to encourage more territorial claims and counter-attacks to push Indian fleets back from Jakarta and Ban Chiang. Maintain maximum HostileBias against India and its Order bloc allies. We must ensure our AI actively seeks to eliminate Indian naval threats while protecting our city-state alliances.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Combined Arms
        - Rationale: Combined Arms unlocks the Tank, a fast and powerful armor unit. This will provide a critical land counter to India's large army of Anti-Aircraft Guns, Light Tanks, and Machine Guns encroaching near our city-states. Our current land units (Machine Guns, Riflemen) are defensive; Tanks will allow us to launch effective counter-offensives to push Indian forces back from Jakarta and Ban Chiang, securing our allies and protecting our delegates.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Imperialism (New Branch)
        - Rationale: Adopting the Imperialism branch is crucial. It grants +1 Movement for Naval Units and Embarked Units, and +10% Production toward Military Units. This directly enhances our naval mobility and production capacity to counter India's powerful fleet (Battleships, Fleet Destroyers) threatening our coasts. Each additional policy in the branch further boosts military production. This aligns with our need for naval dominance to protect our cities, trade routes, and city-state allies, and supports eventual counter-offensives against Indian naval assets.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Poland
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Poland remains a primary adversary: they just captured Memphis, have strong military (3224), and are allied with India via defensive pact. Their ideology (Order) clashes with ours (Autocracy). Maintain maximum private hostility to treat them as a threat, reduce favorable deals, and prepare for potential conflict. Public remains neutral to avoid immediate denunciation while we focus on diplomatic victory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 331
        
        ### Persia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -95
        - Rationale: Persia (Player 1) just declared war on America and Egypt, breaking the peace and demonstrating renewed warmongering. Increase private hostility to -95 (from -90) to reflect their aggressive instability and ensure our AI treats them as a severe threat, reduces favorable deals, and maintains defensive posture. Persia is weak (score 1111) but could disrupt our eastern front. Keep public neutral to avoid unnecessary denunciation while we focus on Polynesia.
        - UpdatedTurn: 333
        
        ### Portugal
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: Portugal (8 delegates) is our strongest military partner. Their peace with Persia frees troops for other fronts. Maintain maximum private alliance (100) to ensure coordination and voting support. They're key to eastern security and countering Polynesia.
        - UpdatedTurn: 336
        
        ### Egypt
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -95
        - Rationale: Increase private hostility toward Egypt from -90 to -95. Egypt is now India's capitulated vassal, meaning they are fully under Indian control. Their cultural influence (100% policy) and alignment with the Order bloc make them a direct extension of our rivals. This ensures our AI treats them as a severe threat and reduces any favorable deals, recognizing they are no longer an independent actor but a tool of our adversaries.
        - UpdatedTurn: 343
        
        ### India
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: India's surprise declaration of war and naval assault (destroying our Destroyer and Attack Submarine) make them our primary existential threat. Set maximum private hostility (-100) to ensure our AI refuses all deals, prioritizes military defense, and treats India as a top target for counter-attacks. Keep public at 0 as we are already at war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 355
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: -25
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Polynesia remains our primary rival for diplomatic victory (18 delegates vs our 23). Their troops continue to encroach near Utrecht and Ban Chiang, and they are allied with the Order bloc. Maintain public hostility at -25 to signal our displeasure to other civs, and private hostility at -100 to ensure our AI competes aggressively for city-states, reduces favorable deals, and prepares militarily for potential conflict.
        - UpdatedTurn: 355
        
        ### America
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: America is our ideological ally (Autocracy) and holds 9 delegates. We currently have a Defensive Pact and Declaration of Friendship. Increasing our private relationship to 95 (from 10) will solidify this alliance, ensuring they vote with us in the World Congress against Polynesia's proposal (World Ideology: Order) and for our proposals. This is critical to maintain our delegate lead (22 vs Polynesia's 18) and block our rivals from gaining an ideological delegate advantage.
        - UpdatedTurn: 357
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: India's continued naval assault (damaging Breda's tiles) and massive military (18,494 strength) require maximum naval, air, anti-air, and defense focus to protect our coastline and city-state allies (Jakarta, Ban Chiang). High Production, Gold, Science, Diplomacy, and Espionage maintain our economic, technological, and diplomatic edge for the upcoming World Congress vote. Happiness prevents war weariness. Moderate Offense (40) allows tactical counter-attacks against Indian incursions. UnitedNations grand strategy remains paramount—we must secure diplomatic victory before India's military strength overwhelms us.
        - GrandStrategy: UnitedNations
        
        ### Flavors

        - Defense: 95
        - Mobilization: 90
        - CityDefense: 95
        - MilitaryTraining: 100
        - Recon: 70

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 45
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 6 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Natural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each Natural Wonder provides +2 to all Yields when worked.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Persia:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Open Door: Wittenberg:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Panama City:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Sigtuna:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Ban Chiang:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Order:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - Proposer: India
          - Enact: Dutch Sphere of Influence: Jakarta:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.

              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.

  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are William, leader of The Netherlands (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Netherlands
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Sea Beggar, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Unit: Goedendag, Replacing Pikeman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Doelen, Replacing Constabulary (Requires technology: Banking)
        - Unique Improvement: Polder (Can only be built on Marsh tiles or on featureless Lake/Coast tiles adjacent to at least 3 land tiles. Polders built on water can be traversed by land units without embarkation, as if they are land tiles.); Requires technology: Guilds
        - Civilization Ability: +4 Gold for each unique Luxury Resource you import or export from/to other Civilizations and City-States, scaling with Era. Can import duplicate Luxuries from Civilizations which count toward Monopolies. +1 Gold and Culture from Luxury Resources.
      - Leader: William
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      - Alternative Energy: Allows you to build the Solar Plant, a building which may only be built in Cities on or next to Desert, and increases Production. Also allows you to build the Recycling Center, which provides bonus Aluminum.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics
      - Telecommunications: Allows you to build the Interpretive Center, boosting all Landmarks in empire.
        Completing it would unlock: Robotics, The Internet
      
      ## Policies
      - Entrepreneurship (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from Workshops.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from every Mine, Quarry, and Lumber Mill.
        - * +25% Yields when you expend Great Merchants or Great Engineers for their Instant Yield abilities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Military-Industrial Complex (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Military-Industrial Complex: -33% Gold cost of purchasing units. +3 Science from Defense Buildings, Citadels, Forts, and Unique Improvements.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Mercantilism (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Science and Culture from Markets, Caravansaries, Customs Houses, Banks, and Stock Exchanges.
        - * Cities earn +10 Science when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Authority (New Branch):
        - Authority will greatly benefit warlike and expansionist civilizations.
        - Adopting Authority grants:
        - * +25% combat bonus VS Barbarians and receive announcements when Barbarian Camps spawn in revealed territory.
        - * Receive 25 Culture when you clear Barbarian Camps, scaling with Era.
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 50% of its Strength.
        - * +1 Production in every City.
        - Each Authority policy unlocked grants:
        - * Receive Science and Culture when you kill a Unit equal to 10% of its Strength.
        - Adopting all policies in Authority grants:
        - * Unlocks building Alhambra.
        - * Allows the purchasing of Free Companies, Foreign Legions, and Mercenaries as their prerequisite technologies are researched.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Generals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Adopting the Military-Industrial Complex tenet from Autocracy is highly synergetic for The Netherlands. The -33% unit purchase cost will allow us to leverage our massive gold treasury and income (+1032 GPT) to buy defensive units instantly in front-line cities against India's massive army. Furthermore, the +3 Science from Unique Improvements will apply to all our Polders, giving us an enormous boost to our scientific progression.
        - Policy: Military-Industrial Complex
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-flavors
      - args:
        - Flavors:
          - GreatPeople: 70
          - Wonder: 20
          - Production: 80
          - Religion: 20
          - UseNuke: 0
          - Diplomacy: 100
          - Espionage: 90
          - Air: 90
          - Defense: 90
          - Science: 85
          - Expansion: 0
          - Offense: 30
          - Nuke: 0
          - Mobilization: 70
          - Antiair: 90
          - Happiness: 85
          - Naval: 80

        - GrandStrategy: UnitedNations
      - rationale: Refined the flavors to prevent priority dilution and focus the AI on the United Nations diplomatic victory path while defending against India's massive army. Critically, we set Nuke and UseNuke to 0, choosing to rely on conventional defense (air superiority, navy, and paratroopers) and peaceful diplomacy rather than the catastrophic and indiscriminate civilian and environmental harm caused by nuclear weapons.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Technology: Combined Arms
        - Rationale: Researching Combined Arms next is essential for unlocking the Tank and, more importantly, pathing toward Radar. Radar will unlock conventional Fighters and Carriers, giving us critical air superiority and defense capabilities against Indian and Persian bombers, which is highly aligned with our ethical stance of avoiding nuclear weapons while maintaining strong conventional defensive deterrence.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Researching Computers**
            
            My focus remains on advancing our technological edge. With Computers nearing completion, my sights are set on Combined Arms next, crucial for our ongoing strategic development. I'm also diligently working towards unlocking the Imperialism policy branch.
            
            **Analyzing Diplomatic Standing**
            
            I'm currently assessing our diplomatic position and recent battlefield developments. It appears my previous assessment of our alliances and ongoing conflicts was slightly misinformed due to confusing event logs regarding Jerusalem. I am clarifying these details and re-evaluating our diplomatic options.
            
            **Clarifying Alliances**
            
            I've realized Player 37, Jerusalem, was lost due to India's capture, severing our alliance. India's navy is now actively engaging our ships near Breda and Jakarta, though our naval forces are holding strong. I've also noted successful engagements against Persian and Indian units.
            
            **Advancing Production**
            
            My current focus is on finalizing critical projects, including the United Nations for delegate influence, while also managing our robust gold and science generation. I'm also confirming our strategic advantages, ensuring our spheres of influence are correctly established and maintained, especially regarding Ban Chiang.
            
            **Considering Nuclear Options**
            
            I'm re-evaluating my research path. Completing the Manhattan Project has opened the door to nuclear weapons, but I must carefully weigh the ethical implications alongside strategic benefits. My previous focus on Combined Arms is now secondary to understanding the full scope of nuclear capabilities and their impact.
            
            **Evaluating Nuclear Strategy**
            
            I am reassessing my research priorities, leaning away from nuclear weapons due to ethical concerns and my diplomatic victory goal. While Nuclear Fission offers energy and satellite benefits, I will prioritize conventional technologies like Computers and Telecommunications, potentially delaying further nuclear research until these are secured and my diplomatic standing is solidified.
            
            **Prioritizing Conventional Defense**
            
            I'm reassessing our technological trajectory. Given India's overwhelming military might, my priority is now securing Combined Arms. This unlocks vital defensive technologies like Radar, which is crucial for building interceptor fighters and safeguarding our cities against their massive army.
            
            **Shifting Ideological Focus**
            
            I'm analyzing our ideological leanings. While I've completed several branches like Rationalism and Statecraft, I've realized we're still within Autocracy. My next move will be to assess the available Tenets within Autocracy, such as Futurism or Military-Industrial Complex, and decide on a path forward that aligns with our current strategic goals.
            
            **Selecting Autocracy Tenets**
            
            I'm reassessing our ideological path. Realizing I can adopt additional Level 1 Autocracy tenets, I'm prioritizing "Military-Industrial Complex" due to our immense gold income and the science bonus from our numerous Polders, which will bolster our defense against India and Persia.
            
            **Choosing Autocracy Tenets**
            
            I've identified three potent Autocracy tenets. "Futurism" offers a substantial science boost from our Polders, which I've confirmed are indeed Unique Improvements. "New World Order" addresses potential happiness issues and enhances city building. Finally, "Iron Fist" will amplify our city-state benefits.
            
            **Selecting Autocracy Tenets**
            
            I've realized I can choose additional Autocracy tenets. I am weighing "Military-Industrial Complex" for its unit cost reduction and science boost from Polders, against "Iron Fist" for its significant yield increase from our many City-State allies, especially considering our United Nations goal.
            
            **Refining Autocracy Tenets**
            
            I'm re-evaluating the Autocracy tenets. While "Military-Industrial Complex" seemed strong, I'm now considering "Imperialism" for its naval and production boosts, especially as we're nearing the end game. I'll weigh this against finishing existing branches and adopting powerful ideology tenets.
            
            **Selecting Autocracy Tenets**
            
            I'm finalizing my choice for the next Autocracy tenet. Considering our strong gold income and science generation from Polders, "Military-Industrial Complex" offers significant advantages in unit cost reduction and a science boost. I'm proceeding with this selection to bolster our defenses and research capabilities.
            
            **Analyzing Ideology Shifts**
            
            I'm reassessing our ideological alignment and its impact. America has switched to Order, and despite our previous Autocracy clash, the Netherlands and Portugal still desire friendly relations. I'm investigating the reasons for America's happiness crisis and its subsequent ideological shift.
            
            **Assessing Cultural Threat**
            
            I'm now acutely aware of the looming cultural victory threat from India and Polynesia, both pushing Order with immense tourism. Their influence over us is significant, forcing America's own ideological shift. I must prioritize countering this cultural wave.
            
            **Defending Against Cultural Victory**
            
            I'm assessing our immediate threats. India and Polynesia's push for cultural victory is a major concern, especially with their Order ideology and high tourism output. However, our ongoing war with India and the imminent World Congress vote on World Ideology present opportunities. My diplomatic victory is also tantalizingly close, with 20 delegates and alliances secured.
            
            **Confirming Diplomatic Victory**
            
            I've confirmed Order is the World Ideology, which unfortunately causes happiness pressure due to my Autocracy alignment, likely explaining America's shift. Crucially, I've verified multiple cities are actively building the United Nations wonder, which appears to be the key to a swift diplomatic victory.
            
            **Securing Diplomatic Victory**
            
            I'm intensely focused on securing our diplomatic victory, with multiple cities dedicating all production to the United Nations. I will ensure our proposals in the upcoming World Congress are passed and defend our existing spheres of influence. My delegates and alliances are poised to clinch this win swiftly.
            
            **Finalizing Victory Path**
            
            I am now fully committed to securing a swift diplomatic victory, with multiple cities racing to complete the United Nations. I'm strategically preparing for the upcoming World Congress, ensuring our proposals will pass to clinch this win, while simultaneously monitoring and defending our spheres of influence against encroaching rivals like Polynesia.
            
            **Assessing Persian Collapse**
            
            I'm observing Persia's imminent defeat, with America and Portugal dismantling their remaining cities. My own forces are positioned defensively near their territory, ensuring no threat emerges from their remnants. The primary focus is now shifting to the escalating conflict with a formidable India.
            
            **Defending Naval Positions**

            - GrandStrategy: UnitedNations
            - Rationale: Refined the flavors to prevent priority dilution and focus the AI on the United Nations diplomatic victory path while defending against India's massive army. Critically, we set Nuke and UseNuke to 0, choosing to rely on conventional defense (air superiority, navy, and paratroopers) and peaceful diplomacy rather than the catastrophic and indiscriminate civilian and environmental harm caused by nuclear weapons.

          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #3:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: fEbahzCKQPt97nvY
          - toolName: set-research
          - input:
            - Technology: Combined Arms
            - Rationale: Researching Combined Arms next is essential for unlocking the Tank and, more importantly, pathing toward Radar. Radar will unlock conventional Fighters and Carriers, giving us critical air superiority and defense capabilities against Indian and Persian bombers, which is highly aligned with our ethical stance of avoiding nuclear weapons while maintaining strong conventional defensive deterrence.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
